## P1-1 terrain geometry, explanation: sand under the foot on slopes (2026-11-24)

Status: measured in this session, unchecked. Definitions: the block above ("definitions fixed before running"). Slides: [site/T3/index.html](../site/T3/index.html). Data: `logs/t3/final_1/d50/flow_report/slope_flow.json`.

<b>Final rerun, 2026-11-24, not yet independently checked.</b> MJWarp <code>3qymp37c</code>, RFT <code>6t0h2ud8</code> and MPM <code>aga5ovkp</code> at their final checkpoints (iteration 19,999), all three in every workflow and all workflows from one code bundle. Beach sand (density 1800 kg/m³, friction 0.80, no cohesion), 1.0 m/s command, slopes −30° to +30° in 5° steps. This slide is built from the 0.50 m bed, the same bed as the headline sweep: 10 trials per policy and slope (seeds 41 and 42, 5 trials each, because 10 trials at this depth do not fit in the memory of one L40). The stance definition, the close-up rule, the failure rule and the depth test were written down before the runs (task record, "definitions fixed before running").

<b>Why the 0.50 m bed.</b> On the 0.35 m bed the loaded feet come within 2 cm of the rigid base at +30° (in 36 of 40 MPM trials and 33 of 40 RFT trials) and for MJWarp from +20°. On the 0.50 m bed they do not: one trial of 120 at +30° (MPM, 1.9 cm) and none of RFT. Between 0.35 and 0.50 m no RFT cell and one MPM cell (−30°, velocity error 0.36 to 0.28 m/s) changed by more than the tolerance fixed beforehand; 12 MJWarp cells changed, always for the worse on the deeper bed. Tables: block "Bed depth" of the geometry slide.

<b>In one paragraph.</b> H1 holds: the sand under a loaded foot moves downhill by an amount that grows with the slope, under every policy. H2 holds against MJWarp at every uphill slope from +10° and against RFT at +30° only; at +25° MPM and RFT are not separated (40 against 39 of 40 trials on this bed). Under MPM's sole the sand moves about a third less than under RFT's at +20° and +25°, although MPM loads the foot harder. Downhill MPM is not better: every policy reaches the goal and MPM's velocity error is the largest.

<b>Supported.</b> After the foot has dug in, the sand that lay within 3 cm under the sole moves downhill during the rest of the stance by about 0 cm on level ground (MJWarp's foot drags it 1.2 cm forward), then at +10°, +20° and +30° by 2.0, 5.0 and 10.8 cm under MJWarp, 1.4, 3.6 and 6.6 cm under RFT and 1.0, 2.2 and 5.2 cm under MPM. Downhill it is 2.0–3.1 cm at −10°, 4.6–6.3 cm at −20° and 10.7–12.1 cm at −30° (range over the policies). The rank correlation between slope angle and this motion over trials is 0.92 (MJWarp), 0.97 (RFT) and 0.97 (MPM), uphill and downhill alike.

Unlike on the 0.25 m bed of the first pass, the policies do not move the sand equally: uphill the order is MJWarp, RFT, MPM at every slope. The earlier statement that the sand motion is "the same for every policy" was a result of the shallow bed, where the feet stood near the base, and is withdrawn.

The foot is what moves the sand. Around each footprint 21–30 L of sand move more than 1 cm per stance on level ground and 32–35 L at +30°. In the control patch 0.6 m to the outer side of the foot nothing moves between −10° and +25° (0.05 L at most); at −30° 3.7–4.1 L move there, so on the steepest downhill slope the bed itself is close to sliding.

<b>Supported uphill against MJWarp, and against RFT at +30° only.</b> Goals of 10 on the 0.50 m bed at +10°, +15°, +20°, +25°, +30°: MJWarp 4, 0, 0, 0, 0; RFT 10, 10, 10, 10, 3; MPM 10, 10, 10, 10, 10. With 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48): MJWarp 0 of 40 from +15°; RFT 40, 40, 39 and 13 of 40 at +15°, +20°, +25° and +30°; MPM 40 of 40 at each. Fisher's exact test, MPM against RFT: p = 1 at +25° (not separated), p = 2e-11 at +30° (separated). The same holds on the 0.35 m bed (37 against 40 at +25°, p = 0.24; 10 against 40 at +30°).

<b>Velocity error (all trials).</b> Uphill MPM tracks best: 0.06–0.07 m/s up to +20° and 0.13 m/s at +30°, against 0.10–0.13 and 0.22 m/s for RFT. Downhill MPM tracks worst at the steep end: 0.28, 0.20 and 0.14 m/s at −30°, −25° and −20°, against 0.22, 0.14 and 0.10 m/s for RFT and 0.23, 0.15 and 0.10 m/s for MJWarp. Every policy reaches the goal on every downhill slope.

<b>MPM's foot does not move less, the sand under it does.</b> The bearing point of the sole moves downhill per stance by 4.5 cm (RFT) and 4.2 cm (MPM) at +20°, and 4.0 and 6.1 cm at +25°. Of that, the sand under the sole carries 3.6 cm (RFT) against 2.2 cm (MPM) at +20° and 4.5 against 3.4 cm at +25°; after digging in, MPM's foot slides 3.1–3.5 cm over that sand at +15° to +25°, RFT's 0.3–0.6 cm at +15° and +20°.

<b>How MJWarp fails.</b> Its 47 failed uphill trials on this bed are 25 collapses (pelvis too low), 19 falls forward, 2 falls sideways and 1 lane exit. "Leaves the lane sideways", the main failure on the 0.25 m bed, was a result of standing on the base there. RFT's 7 failures at +30° are 6 falls forward and 1 lane exit.

<b>Does foot behaviour explain the smaller foot slip of RFT and MPM?</b> Yes, through the sole angle, not through step timing or loading. The bearing point of MJWarp's sole covers 0.53–1.03 m/s while the foot is loaded on every slope, level ground included; for RFT and MPM it covers 0.11–0.16 m/s on level ground and rises to 0.84 and 0.70 m/s at +30°. So the rigid-trained policy's foot never comes to rest on the sand, while the others plant the foot and lose that hold only on steep slopes.

<b>Sole angle.</b> MJWarp holds the sole 24° toes-down already on level ground, 44° at +10° and 57° at +20° (37° below level): it bears on the toe edge, which ploughs. RFT and MPM hold it within 2° of level on level ground and 6° and 8° below level at +20°. At +30° both are about 20° below level (51° and 48° toes-down to the slope).

<b>Step timing.</b> On level ground MJWarp steps fastest (step time 0.22 s, RFT 0.26 s, MPM 0.30 s) with the shortest steps. Uphill from +10° all three step in 0.19–0.24 s with 0.20–0.25 m steps, so stance duration, step length and step time do not separate the policies where the goals do.

<b>Loading.</b> Peak load per stance at +20°: 2.0 body weights (MJWarp), 1.1 (RFT), 1.9 (MPM). MPM loads the foot about as hard as MJWarp and still keeps its hold, so a softer touchdown is not the explanation either. Uphill, load comes onto the foot within about one physics step (5 ms) for every policy.

At +20° the median trial of RFT and of MPM reached the goal; MJWarp's ended with the pelvis too low. MJWarp's loaded sole stands 57° toes-down to the slope with the toe buried; RFT and MPM hold it 26° and 28° toes-down, which is 6° and 8° below level on a 20° slope. In every row the sand downhill of the loaded foot has moved several centimetres downhill and the sand uphill of it has barely moved. The frames differ in stance phase because the frame is chosen by rule, 0.1 s after a foot first takes load.

<b>Rigid slopes (same slopes, no sand, same bundle, 10 trials).</b> Between −20° and +15° every policy reaches 10 of 10 on rigid ground. At the extremes each policy is best on the ground it trained on. At +30°: MJWarp 9 of 10 on rigid ground and 0 of 10 on sand; RFT 8 and 3; MPM 0 and 10. At −30°: MJWarp 9 and 10; RFT 7 and 10; MPM 5 (velocity error 0.70 m/s) and 10 (0.28 m/s). So MPM is not the better walker everywhere: it is the better walker on sand, and the worst on steep rigid ground.

<b>Sliding angle of the sand.</b> Unloaded beach sand has no sharp sliding angle in this simulation: it creeps, and by the 1 cm/s rule it slides at 35° (friction 0.80). All slopes of the sweep are below that, which fits the still control patch up to +25°.

<b>Limits.</b> One training seed and one checkpoint per policy. Ten trials per cell here (40 at +15° to +30°); the start states on the 0.50 m bed are not the same ten as on the other beds. MJWarp's results still depend on the bed depth: on every soft or steep cell it does worse on the deeper bed, so its numbers on a deeper bed could be worse still where it has not already failed.

Proposal for one single-column figure for P1. Top row: (a) goals reached and (b) velocity error against slope, −30° to +30°, three policies, 0.50 m bed. Middle row: (c) sand under the sole moving downhill per stance against slope (H1, and MPM's line lowest uphill) and (d) sole angle to the slope in mid-stance with the level-sole line (how MJWarp differs). Bottom row: the material ladder on level ground, velocity error and one behaviour quantity against the materials from hard ground to loose poppy seed.

What it would not show: the rigid-ground sweep (each policy best on its own ground), which deserves one sentence in the text, and the bed-depth dependence of MJWarp, which belongs in the limitations.

## P2 object towing: material not shown on the slides, kept for later

Two tests were taken off `site/T3/index.html` and the published copy on 2026-11-23 at Junnosuke's request (the
P3 slide was too crowded and the confining channel will not be used in the paper). Nothing was deleted: data,
scripts, figures and videos are where they were, and the last page that showed them is kept as
`site/T3/p3_channel_not_shown_20261123.html`. The P3 slide now shows goal reaching with the tether-cut control,
the force figure and the short deformation result only.

### A. Controlled box test in a sand channel (2026-11-22)

Status: measured in this session, unchecked. The paper figure
`figures/t3_tow.pdf` still uses this test as its right panel; `figures/t3_tow_v2.pdf` is the version without it.

**Question.** Does the sand a box has displaced change the force on it?

**Setup.** A 15 kg cube (0.3 m, weight 147 N) on the T1 probe's fixed MPM bed, free to sink under its own
weight but held level, dragged at 0.5 m/s 4.8 m out and 4.8 m back through its own track. Bed 6.4 m long and
either 0.36 m wide (the sand cannot pass the cube) or 1.6 m wide. One run per bed.

**Result.** Force averaged over 1.5–2.5 m ("early") and 3.5–4.5 m ("late") of each pass.

| Bed | Pass | Force early → late [N] | Depth early → late [cm] | Pile in front at the end [cm] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| narrow, 0.36 m | out, fresh sand | 703 → 1052 | 7.3 → 7.0 | 20.8 |
| narrow, 0.36 m | back, own track | 668 → 768 | 10.3 → 9.3 | 16.3 |
| wide, 1.6 m | out, fresh sand | 557 → 756 | 13.9 → 16.2 | 12.7 |
| wide, 1.6 m | back, own track | 464 → 609 | 16.7 → 16.7 | 9.7 |

In the narrow bed the force rises by half at constant depth and speed while a pile builds in front of the cube;
in the wide bed most of the raised sand lies beside the path (23 L beside, 13 L in front; narrow bed 7 L and
16 L). The pass through its own track needs less force in both beds.

**Limits.** The cube is held level, so it ploughs: the force is 3 to 7 times its weight, far above the roughly
105 N on the cube towed by G1, which is free to tilt and climb. These are not towing forces. Only 25 of the
120 L missing from the trench in the narrow bed reappear above the surface.

**Files** (unified worktree). Scripts `scripts/benchmarks/t3/box_channel.py`, `box_channel_figure.py`. Data
`logs/t3/t3-box-channel-2/t3/box_channel/` (OSMO workflow `t3-box-channel-2`). Figures and numbers
`logs/t3/box_channel_fig/box_channel.png`, `box_channel_map.png`, `box_channel.json`. To show it again, pass
`--box-channel logs/t3/box_channel_fig/box_channel.png` to `site_slides.py`.

### B. G1 tows the cube along a sand channel (2026-11-22 and 23)

Status: measured in this session, unchecked. MPM-trained tow policy, 15 kg cube unless stated, 16 trials per
row, 1.0 m/s command unless stated.

**Question.** What happens when the sand the cube displaces cannot leave sideways and G1 keeps pulling?

**Setup.** The towing test with two walls 0.36 m apart along the tow line. The walls act on the sand only; robot
and cube pass through them, and a guide force (4 to 6 N on average) keeps the cube on the line. "Open bed" is the
same run without walls. Force and depth are averages over 8 to 11 s; depth is the cube's bottom below the
settled sand surface, pile is the sand at the cube's front face above that surface.

| Cube | Bed | Goals | Cube travel [m] | Ground force on cube [N] | Tether tension [N] | Pile [cm] | Depth [cm] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| free, 15 kg, 1.0 m/s | channel | 16 / 16 | 9.4 | 115 | 126 | 8.4 | 0.8 |
| free, 15 kg, 1.0 m/s | open | 16 / 16 | 9.5 | 103 | 115 | 6.5 | 1.8 |
| free, 15 kg, 0.5 m/s | channel | 16 / 16 | 9.5 | 96 | 109 | 6.1 | 0.9 |
| free, 15 kg, 0.5 m/s | open | 16 / 16 | 9.5 | 93 | 104 | 5.0 | 2.2 |
| held level, 15 kg | channel | 15 / 16 | 1.4 | 122 | 138 | 4.7 | 7.8 |
| held level, 15 kg | open | 15 / 16 | 1.5 | 135 | 151 | 3.9 | 10.2 |
| free, 20 kg | channel | 15 / 16 | 8.5 | 155 | 170 | 7.5 | 2.1 |
| free, 20 kg | open | 16 / 16 | 8.9 | 166 | 183 | 5.4 | 5.4 |
| free, 25 kg | channel | 13 / 16 | 3.0 | 161 | 177 | 4.8 | 5.5 |
| free, 25 kg | open | 9 / 16 | 4.4 | 168 | 184 | 3.4 | 6.9 |
| free, 30 kg | channel | 13 / 16 | 0.7 | 122 | 136 | 2.6 | 9.5 |
| free, 30 kg | open | 15 / 16 | 0.6 | 106 | 119 | 1.7 | 11.1 |

"Goals" counts the robot's path length, which also grows when it rocks in place, so for the level and the heavy
cubes the cube travel is the number to read.

**Result.** With the free 15 kg cube the channel raises the pull by about a tenth at 1.0 m/s (115 against 103 N)
and by 4 % at 0.5 m/s. More sand collects in front of the cube, but the cube is free to rise and rides over it
(0.8 cm deep against 1.8 cm). Held level, or made heavier, the cube digs in (8 to 11 cm) and G1 stalls after
0.6 to 1.5 m; the channel makes little difference then. The tether rises at about 25° and carries about 50 N of
the cube's 147 N weight; the free cube tilts about 6°.

**Limits.** The walls are not physical for the robot or the cube. One training seed, one checkpoint.

**Files** (unified worktree). Scripts `scripts/benchmarks/t3/tow_channel.py`, `tow_channel_figure.py`,
`sand_pile_figure.py`. Data `logs/t3/t3-tow-channel-4` (channel and open, 1.0 and 0.5 m/s), `-7` (free and level
with particle snapshots), `-8` (box-only videos and particles, level video), `-9` (20, 25, 30 kg), `-10`
(closer-camera videos). Figures and numbers `logs/t3/tow_channel_fig/` (`tow_channel.png`,
`tow_channel_across.png`, `tow_channel_level.png`, the `.json` files, and `free/`, `level/`, `mass20/`,
`mass25/`, `mass30/`), `logs/t3/sand_pile_fig/`, videos `logs/t3/tow_channel_video/`. To show it again, pass
`--tow-channel logs/t3/tow_channel_fig/tow_channel.png --tow-channel-video logs/t3/tow_channel_video
--tow-level logs/t3/tow_channel_fig/tow_channel_level.png --sand-pile logs/t3/sand_pile_fig/sand_pile.png
--tow-mass logs/t3/tow_channel_fig --sand-pile-mass logs/t3/sand_pile_fig/sand_pile_mass.png` to `site_slides.py`.

## Superseded: P2 object towing, the start-up peak in the ground force on the cube (2026-11-23)

**Superseded on 2026-11-24.** The peak below (1.15 to 1.21 times the steady force) was measured on the tow
checkpoint of iteration 30,500. On the final tow checkpoint (39,998; T2 logs `logs/t2/tow_d25`, `tow_d35`,
`tow_d45`; MPM-trained tow policy on MPM sand, 16 trials each) the peak of the mean ground force in the first
4 s against its mean from 5 to 10 s is, at the 1.0 m/s command, 1.11 on the 0.25 m bed (116 against 105 N),
1.02 on 0.35 m (122 against 120 N) and 1.08 on 0.45 m (132 against 122 N); at the 0.5 m/s command 1.11, 1.00
and 1.01. Recomputed in this session with the same definition; the independent check of the towing rerun
(`checks/T2.md`) finds the same three values at 1.0 m/s. So on the final checkpoint there is no start-up peak
worth a statement, and the peak is not used on the slides or in the paper. The text below is kept as a record.


Status: read from existing logs, no new runs, unchecked. MPM-trained tow policy, 15 kg cube, 1.0 m/s, 16
trials each: the towing evaluation on MPM sand and on rigid ground (`logs/t2/tow_eval2`), and the open-bed run
of the channel test (`logs/t3/t3-tow-channel-4`), the only towing log that recorded the sand surface. Script
`scripts/benchmarks/t3/tow_startup_figure.py`; figure and numbers `logs/t3/tow_startup/` (unified worktree).

| Quantity (mean over trials, 0.2 s average) | Towing evaluation, MPM sand | Open bed, MPM sand | Rigid ground |
|---|---|---|---|
| Peak ground force in the first 4 s [N], at time [s] | 119 at 2.38 | 125 at 2.34 | 55 at 2.44 |
| Steady ground force, 5–10 s [N] | 103 | 103 | 50 |
| Peak / steady | 1.15 | 1.21 | 1.11 |
| Cube speed at the peak / steady [m/s] | 0.73 / 0.91 | 0.65 / 0.90 | 1.01 / 1.00 |
| Cube centre below its rest level on the settled surface, at the peak / steady [cm] | 3.5 / 1.8 | 3.9 / 1.8 | — |
| Cube tilt at the peak / largest (time) / steady [deg] | 8.1 / 8.7 (2.54 s) / 5.9 | 6.2 / 8.5 (2.78 s) / 5.6 | 1.9 / 2.7 (0.84 s) / 1.8 |
| Sand at the front face above the settled surface, at 1 s / at the peak / at 4 s / steady [cm] | not recorded | 2.1 / 5.6 / 6.2 / 6.4 | — |
| Drift of the force from 4 s to the end [N/s] | −0.17 (4.0–11.3 s) | −0.14 (4.0–11.3 s) | −0.10 (4.0–10.2 s) |

**Reading.** The peak comes with the cube digging in: at the moment of the peak the cube is at its deepest
(3.5 to 3.9 cm below its rest level, against 1.8 cm in steady towing) and at its slowest (0.65 to 0.73 m/s,
against 0.9). It is not the jerk of the tether going taut, which happens 1.5 s earlier (tension first reaches
100 to 120 N at about 0.8 s; on rigid ground 82 N at 0.64 s). After that jerk the cube overshoots to about
1.05 m/s, the tension eases to about 95 N between 1.2 and 1.9 s, and the cube sinks and slows; the force peak is
where the tether pulls it out again (tension 134 to 140 N at 2.4 s). It is also not a pile that builds and then
goes away: the sand at the front face is still rising at the peak (5.6 cm) and stays at 6.2 to 6.4 cm afterwards.
The tilt is largest slightly after the peak. The logs hold the size of the tilt but not its direction, so
nose-down cannot be told from nose-up. The depth is that of the cube's centre, which tilting alone would raise,
not lower, so the extra 2 cm are real sinking; the lowest edge may be up to 2 cm deeper than this number at 8.5°.

**Steady force.** It does not drift upward: the straight-line fit from 4 s to the last sample with at least
half of the trials running falls by 0.14 to 0.17 N/s on MPM sand (about 1 N over 7 s).

## Superseded: do the loaded feet reach the rigid base under the sand? (2026-11-24, first pass)

**Superseded by the final rerun of 2026-11-24.** This block describes the first pass (MPM at iteration 16,500,
0.25 and 0.35 m beds) and uses "within 2 cm of the base" as a validity rule. The final rerun tested that rule on
0.25, 0.35 and 0.50 m beds and dropped it: it does not pick out the cells that change with bed depth (block
"P1-1 terrain geometry", part "Bed depth"). The clearance itself is still reported for every cell there. The base
height in the numbers below is also wrong near the edge of the start platform, as the independent check found;
runs from the final rerun on read the mesh height under each sole corner. Kept as a record.


Status: read from the logs of the walking evaluation, unchecked. Results in which the feet reach the base are results on rigid ground and are invalid as material or slope results. Script `scripts/benchmarks/t3/base_clearance.py`; numbers `logs/t3/base_clearance/base_clearance.json`.

**Rule.** For every loaded sample the height of the lowest of the four sole corners above the rigid base is taken (the base is the terrain mesh lowered by the bed depth). The feet stop about 1.3 cm above the base, one layer of sand particles, so a corner within 2 cm counts as standing on the base. Each cell: trials in which that happens, the median over trials of the closest approach, and the share of loaded samples within 2 cm.

## P1-1 terrain geometry: slope sweep on firm sand (2026-11-24)

Status: measured in this session; the recheck of the final rerun is in `checks/T3.md`, the runs made after it are unchecked. Three policies: MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp` (iteration 19,999 (final)). The earlier MPM policy `eayrj79r` is no longer shown because it saw sand only on wave and random rough terrain; its results stay on disk (`logs/t3/eval_5`, `logs/t3/flow_1`).

Uphill, MPM reaches every goal; RFT fails at +30°, MJWarp from +10° to +20°.

Downhill, every policy reaches the goal; MPM tracks the command worst.

The +30° result holds without the start drop and with the sand kept awake.

<b>Final rerun, 2026-11-24, not yet independently checked.</b> Beach sand (1800 kg/m³, friction 0.80, no cohesion), 1.0 m/s command, 13 s, slopes −30° to +30° in 5° steps, 0.50 m bed (the training bed is 0.25 m). 10 trials per policy and slope: seeds 41 and 42 with 5 trials each, because 10 trials on this bed do not fit in the memory of one L40. MJWarp <code>3qymp37c</code>, RFT <code>6t0h2ud8</code> and MPM <code>aga5ovkp</code>, each at its final checkpoint (iteration 19,999), run together in every workflow from one code bundle.

<b>Goals of 10 at +10°, +15°, +20°, +25°, +30°.</b> MJWarp 4, 0, 0, 0, 0; RFT 10, 10, 10, 10, 3; MPM 10 at each. With 40 trials per cell RFT reaches 39 of 40 at +25° and 13 of 40 at +30°, MPM 40 of 40 at each: MPM and RFT are separated at +30° (Fisher's exact test, p = 2e-11) and not at +25° (p = 1). Downhill every policy reaches every goal.

<b>Velocity error of the successful trials.</b> Uphill MPM is lowest (0.06–0.07 m/s up to +20°, 0.13 m/s at +30°; RFT 0.10–0.13 and 0.18 m/s). On steep downhill slopes MPM is highest: 0.28, 0.20 and 0.14 m/s at −30°, −25° and −20°, against 0.22, 0.14 and 0.10 m/s for RFT.

<b>On rigid ground the order reverses</b> (same slopes, no sand, same bundle, 10 trials): at +30° MJWarp reaches 9 of 10, RFT 8 and MPM 0; at −30° MJWarp 9, RFT 7 and MPM 5. Between −20° and +15° all three reach 10 of 10. Each policy is best on the kind of ground it trained on.

<b>Does the result depend on how the test is set up?</b> Three controls on the steep slopes, 40 trials per policy each (table below): the robot started on the settled surface instead of dropped onto it, the same with 6 s settling, and with 12 s settling and all sand near the robot kept awake. MPM reaches 40 of 40 at every slope in every setting. RFT reaches 39 or 40 of 40 up to +25° in every setting and 13, 9, 18 and 1 of 40 at +30°. MJWarp reaches 0 of 40 from +20° in every setting; at +15° it reaches 0 of 40 with the task's sleeping and 23 of 40 with the sand awake, so where MJWarp starts to fail (between +10° and +20°) depends on the setting. RFT's success rate at +30° is not a stable number either: it ranges from 1 to 18 of 40 over the settings, and only its separation from MPM holds in all of them. RFT's velocity error at +25° is 0.16 m/s in the task setting started on the surface and 0.26 m/s with the sand awake, more than the 0.05 m/s tolerance, so no statement on RFT's error at +25° and above is made from one setting. The figures above use the default start, 2.5 s settling and the task's sleeping. The sand is generated with 1.25 particles per cell edge in every run; at that density the bed compacts while it settles (task record), which is a condition of every sinkage and sand-motion number.

<b>What a trial is.</b> Within one run all trials share one settled bed (one template) and differ only in the robot's start: position within ±0.1 m, heading within ±0.1 rad, joint angles within ±10 %. The starts are the same for all three policies. Forty trials are forty starts on that bed, not forty beds.

<b>Limits.</b> One training seed and one checkpoint per policy. The start states on the 0.50 m bed are not the same ten as on the other beds. The robot starts 0.76 m above the level the sand is generated at and the settled surface of this bed lies 10 cm below it, so every trial starts with a 10 cm drop onto the flat start platform, 1.9 m before the slope; on level ground of this bed all 30 trials reach the goal, and the uphill failures happen 3 to 7 m from the start. MJWarp's numbers depend on the bed depth (block below).

<b>Training budget, from the W&B configs.</b> MJWarp and RFT: 1024 environments on each of 4 GPUs, 24 steps per iteration, 20,000 iterations, about 1.97 billion transitions each. MPM: 64 environments on each of 40 GPUs, 24 steps, 20,000 iterations, about 1.23 billion transitions. This reads the configured environment count as per GPU, which is not verified beyond the config. All three use training seed 42.

**Bed depth: why the 0.50 m bed, and what changes with depth.**

The slope sweep and the six sands of the material axis (level ground and +10°) were run on 0.25, 0.35 and 0.50 m beds. Rule fixed before the runs: a cell counts as unchanged between 0.35 and 0.50 m if its goal count differs by at most 3 of 10 and its velocity error by at most 0.05 m/s. Of 75 cells, 13 changed: 12 of MJWarp, 0 of RFT and 1 of MPM (table). MJWarp always does worse on the deeper bed.

The earlier validity rule (a cell is invalid if any trial comes within 2 cm of the rigid base) does not pick out the cells that change: 10 of the 13 changed cells pass it, the changed cells reach up to 14.7 cm of clearance, and 44 unchanged cells lie at or below that. As decided beforehand for this case, clearance is dropped as a validity rule and results are quoted from the deepest bed. For RFT and MPM that bed is deep enough by this test; for MJWarp no bed tested is: where it has not already failed, it could do worse on a deeper bed.

On the 0.35 m bed the feet come within 2 cm of the base at +30° in 36 of 40 MPM trials and 33 of 40 RFT trials; on the 0.50 m bed in 1 of 40 MPM trials (1.9 cm) and no RFT trial. The goal counts at +30° are the same on both beds (RFT 10 and 13 of 40, MPM 40 and 40 of 40).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MJWarp | 2000 kg/m³, friction 0.50 | +0° | 10 → 0 | 0.35 → — | 2.0 → 0.9 |

**Steep uphill slopes with 40 trials per policy: two beds, start with and without the drop, sand asleep and awake.**

Seeds 41 to 44 with 10 trials each on the 0.35 m bed, seeds 41 to 48 with 5 trials each on the 0.50 m bed. "Separated" was defined beforehand as Fisher's exact test on goals giving p < 0.05; the criterion for the controls (MPM against RFT still separated at +30°, each count within 12 of 40 of the default-start count) was written down before they ran. It is met by RFT and MPM in every setting, and by MJWarp except at +15° with the sand awake.

## P1-2 terrain material: from hard ground to loose poppy seed, level ground (2026-11-24)

Status: measured in this session; the recheck of the final rerun is in `checks/T3.md`, the runs made after it are unchecked. Three policies: MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp` (iteration 19,999 (final)). The earlier MPM policy `eayrj79r` is no longer shown because it saw sand only on wave and random rough terrain; its results stay on disk (`logs/t3/eval_5`, `logs/t3/flow_1`).

MPM against MJWarp is supported: MJWarp falls on loose poppy seed in both bed settings.

MPM and RFT reach every goal; RFT's velocity error is 0.03–0.08 m/s higher on every sand.

On the ladder that gap does not grow with softness; on the grid the two settings disagree.

<b>Runs made after the recheck; checked (check 3 in <code>checks/T3.md</code>).</b> Level ground, 1.0 m/s, 0.50 m bed, 42 sands (beach sand with density and friction replaced), 5 trials per sand and policy, three policies per task. Unlike in the earlier grid, every sand is generated and settled as itself, and the robot starts 0.76 m above that sand's settled surface. Setting T (the task's own): 2.5 s settling and the task's sleeping. The same grid in setting W (12 s settling, sand near the robot awake) is in a block below. The figure keeps its earlier style.

<b>Grey crosses: the bed collapses while settling.</b> How far the bed sinks before the run depends on the friction only, not on the density: 8, 12, 15, 21, 25 and 30 cm of the 50 cm bed for friction 0.9, 0.7, 0.5, 0.3, 0.2 and 0.1 in setting T (10, 14, 18, 27, 33 and 38 cm in W; with friction 0.1 most sands could not be run at all). A sand counts as collapsed when its surface lies more than a third of the bed depth below the generated level, a rule fixed before the runs: friction 0.3 and below in T, friction 0.5 and below in W. The threshold was chosen after probing friction 0.1, 0.3 and 0.9 (30, 21 and 8 cm) and before the 15 and 18 cm of friction 0.5 were known, which it ends up dividing. Collapsed sands are not material results, which also covers the earlier "feet reach the base on low friction". The 2 cm marks of the earlier figure are gone with the rule.

<b>On the 14 sands whose bed holds in both settings</b> (friction 0.7 and 0.9, 250 to 3000 kg/m³): MPM reaches 70 of 70 goals in T and in W with a velocity error of 0.05–0.12 m/s per sand; RFT reaches 70 of 70 in both with 0.09–0.23 m/s (T) and 0.09–0.18 m/s (W); MJWarp reaches 54 of 70 (T) and 65 of 70 (W) with errors up to 0.68 and 0.41 m/s. The two settings agree, by the tolerance fixed beforehand, on 14 of 14 sands for MPM, 13 of 14 for RFT and 9 of 14 for MJWarp. RFT's error is above MPM's on every one of these sands. In T the difference grows towards light sand: 0.03–0.05 m/s from 1000 kg/m³ up, 0.05–0.06 at 500 and 0.08–0.13 at 250 kg/m³. In W there is no trend (0.04–0.08 m/s, largest at 3000 kg/m³). The settings disagree, so no statement on a trend is made from the grid; both policies' errors about double at 250 kg/m³, which lies outside the training range. Hard ground for reference: 0.04, 0.05 and 0.04 m/s.

<b>Friction 0.5 holds in setting T only</b> (15 cm in T, 18 cm in W), so it is not among the 14 sands above. In T, on those seven sands, MPM reaches 35 of 35 goals, RFT 34 of 35 (one fall at 1000 kg/m³) and MJWarp 16 of 35. The title of the figure counts the 21 sands that hold in T.

<b>What a trial is.</b> Within one run all trials share one settled bed (one template, terrain and scene seed fixed) and differ only in the robot's start: position within ±0.1 m, heading within ±0.1 rad, joint angles within ±10 %. The grid uses the same five starts on all 42 sands, in both settings and for all three policies, so "70 of 70" is 14 sands times those five starts. Comparisons between policies and between the settings are therefore paired; the spread between trials is small for the same reason and says little about other beds.

<b>Runs made after the recheck; checked (check 3 in <code>checks/T3.md</code>).</b> Level ground only, 1.0 m/s command, 13 s. Grounds fixed before the runs: hard ground (rigid contact); dense sand (3000 kg/m³, friction 0.90); beach sand (1800, 0.80); packed poppy seed (682, 1.07); light sand (1000, 0.70); loose poppy seed (640, 0.73). All sands are cohesionless with rigid grains, generated with 1.25 particles per cell edge. They are ordered by measured foot sinkage, the same order in both settings. The two poppy seeds lie outside the range MPM was trained on. 0.50 m bed, 10 trials per policy and ground (seeds 41 and 42: ten robot starts on one settled bed per run, the same starts for every policy and in both settings), three policies per workflow, robot started 0.76 m above the settled surface, velocity error from 1.0 s over the trials that reached the goal.

<b>Two bed settings, and statements only where they agree.</b> How far a foot sinks in this simulation depends on how the bed was prepared (block below), so the ladder was run twice: in setting T (2.5 s settling, the task's sleeping; shown above) and in setting W (12 s settling, all sand near the robot awake; block below). A statement is made only where both agree within 3 goals of 10 and 0.05 m/s.

<b>What holds in both settings.</b> MPM reaches 10 of 10 goals on every ground with a velocity error of 0.04–0.08 m/s. RFT reaches 10 of 10 on every ground with 0.05 m/s on hard ground and 0.09–0.13 m/s on every sand. MJWarp falls in 10 of 10 trials on loose poppy seed, and on beach and dense sand it reaches every goal. So MPM against MJWarp is supported. MPM against RFT is not separated in goals anywhere; RFT's error is 0.03–0.08 m/s above MPM's on every sand, firm or soft, and 0.01 m/s above on hard ground. That is a small constant gap, not a sign that MPM copes better with softer ground.

<b>What depends on the setting.</b> MJWarp on the two grounds in the middle: on light sand it falls in 10 of 10 trials in T and reaches 10 of 10 in W (with 0.39 m/s error); on packed poppy seed it reaches 9 of 10 with 0.48 m/s in T and 10 of 10 with 0.06 m/s in W. Sinkage is also smaller in W on every sand (dense sand 2.1–3.1 cm against 4.2–5.0 cm; loose poppy seed 15–24 cm against 21–27 cm).

<b>How each policy steps, in both settings.</b> MJWarp's sole tips further toes-down in mid-stance the softer the ground: 14°, 25° and 31° from dense sand to packed poppy seed in T, and 8°, 16°, 24° and 36° from dense to light sand in W. On loose poppy seed it is 58° (T) and 48° (W), but those are angles of a falling robot: every trial there ends within 4 s and the values come from the few steps taken from 1.5 s until 0.5 s before the fall (8 steps in T). Both feet are loaded for more of the time (up to all of it) and the pelvis drops. RFT and MPM keep the sole near level (RFT at most 10° toes-down in T and 6° in W, MPM within about 4° of level) and both shorten the step to 0.22–0.24 s on the softest ground. Their feet sink equally deep. On the three softest grounds MPM keeps the foot on the ground longer than RFT (stance 0.28–0.38 s against 0.25–0.26 s), with a longer double stance (0.05–0.07 s against 0.01–0.03 s); on hard ground, dense sand and, in setting W, beach sand RFT's stance is as long as MPM's or longer. MPM's peak load falls with softness to about 1.15 body weights while RFT's stays at 1.3–1.5.

<b>Answer by the rule fixed beforehand: not explained yet.</b> A quantity was to be named only if it follows the velocity error across the grounds for both baselines (rank correlation of 0.8 or more) while MPM changes it less than half as much. None passes in either setting, because RFT's error does not vary with softness. MJWarp's rising error goes with its toes-down sole and its wading (rank correlation 1.0 in T, 0.7 in W). The earlier observation that MPM keeps a 0.33 s step where the baselines shorten theirs holds on beach sand in setting T (0.30 s against 0.25 and 0.22 s) and not in setting W (0.33 s against 0.32 and 0.28 s); on softer grounds MPM shortens its step too. It depends on the bed and is not the explanation.

**The grid figure with the collapsed beds left out (setting T).**

The same data as the figure above; the surfaces stop at the sands whose bed holds (friction 0.5, 0.7 and 0.9 in setting T).

**The density-friction grid with 12 s settling and the sand near the robot awake (setting W).**

Setting W, 5 trials per sand and policy. Beds with friction 0.5 and below collapse while settling (18 cm or more of 50 cm). On the sands with friction 0.7 and 0.9 see the numbers in the details of the figure above.

**How the bed is prepared decides how far the feet sink.**

A local test after the recheck (MJWarp on dense sand, 0.50 m bed, default start; task record, definitions block) shows two things acting together. The bed keeps losing height while it is awake: its surface lies 8.2, 9.1, 9.7 and 10.0 cm below the generated level after 2.5, 6, 12 and 24 s of settling, although the settling check passes each time. And sleeping freezes the bed in the state it has: on a bed frozen after 2.5 s a foot wakes loose sand that then compacts under it (4.0 cm sinkage, 17 L of sand displaced per step, against 2.4 cm and 4 L with the sand kept awake); on a bed frozen after 6 s or more the sleeping sand gives 0.6–0.8 cm, a third of the 1.7–2.1 cm of awake sand. The earlier ladder with 6 s settling was in that second state ("sand that does not yield") and is withdrawn.

The T1 session traces the compaction to the sampling density (1.25 particles per cell edge leaves 12 to 14 % of the grid cells empty at the start; unchecked, in its record). That would not depend on friction, and it would account for about 6 to 7 cm of a 50 cm bed. The beds here sink 8 cm at friction 0.9 and 30 cm at friction 0.1, the same for every density, so the low-friction collapse needs a second, friction-dependent cause; the bed spreading sideways toward its angle of repose is one candidate, not tested.

Consequence for reading this page: foot sinkage, sand displaced per step and sand motion under the sole are numbers for one bed preparation each. Goal counts and velocity errors of RFT and MPM are the same in both settings on every ground of the ladder; MJWarp's are not.

Snow and clay were not rerun. The first material axis (level ground and +10°, default start) and the ladders with 6 s settling on 0.35, 0.50 and 0.65 m beds are superseded by the runs above; their data stay under <code>logs/t3/final_1</code> and the earlier numbers are in the block "Final-checkpoint rerun of P1" of the task record. The 0.65 m ladder's result on loose poppy seed (MPM 12 of 12, RFT 9 of 12, not separated, p = 0.22) was measured with 6 s settling and is not repeated in the two settings here.

<!-- T3-mixed-terrain:start -->
## Mixed MJWarp/MPM terrain task on the unified code (2026-11-20)

Purpose: the MPM policy `eayrj79r` overspeeds downhill (2.05 m/s at −30°, commanded 1.0 m/s) because the legacy
mixed training gave the sand robots only wave and random rough terrain. The new task gives every rough terrain
type once as rigid MJWarp ground and once as MPM sand, in one training run.

Nothing here is committed. All paths are relative to `~/isaac/worktrees/mpm-unification/IsaacLab` unless stated.

### Task IDs

- `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed` (training)
- `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed-Play`

Both use the standard PPO runner `G1MixedTerrainPPORunnerCfg` by default, as the legacy mixed training did.

### Design

| Item | How it works |
|---|---|
| Terrain | `ROUGH_TERRAINS_CFG` with 10 columns of 8 m. Each of the five terrain types fills two adjacent columns. |
| Ground per column | First column of each pair is rigid, second carries sand. The generated mesh is one rigid MJWarp collider everywhere; in sand columns the sand lies on top of it, so the sand top is one sand depth above the neighbouring rigid surface. |
| Which robots get sand | The first `sand_env_fraction` of the environments (default 0.5). Only these hold the `sand` asset; rigid environments hold no particles at all. This uses Isaac Lab clone combinations, as the legacy mixed task did. |
| Placement | Sand environments are spread over the sand columns, rigid ones over the rigid columns. With 10 environments: envs 0–4 on columns 1, 3, 5, 7, 9 and envs 5–9 on columns 0, 2, 4, 6, 8. With 2 environments both are on the wave pair. |
| Foot contact margin | 2 cm in sand environments, 0 cm in rigid environments, written per environment before the solvers are built. |
| Staying on the own ground | `root_out_of_patch` ends the episode as a time-out when the pelvis comes within 0.5 m of the side of its column or of the end of the tile grid. |
| Spawn | Robots start 5 cm higher than in the sand-only task, because the reset samples the ground height under the pelvis only. |
| MPM storage | Sized for the sand environments only: 41 M active cells at 4096 environments instead of 82 M. |
| Foot contact force | Sand reaction as in the sand-only task, plus the MJWarp ground contact force, so contact flags and gait timers work on rigid ground. |

The two columns of a pair have the same terrain type and the same difficulty band per row, but are not identical
copies: the generator draws the difficulty within the band, and the random rough heights, separately per cell.

### Files changed for the mixed task

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `…/velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/streaming_mpm_mixed_env_cfg.py` (new, 182 lines) | `MixedMPMTerrainImporter`, `MixedMPMTerrainImporterCfg` (`sand_env_fraction`), `G1MixedTerminationsCfg`, `G1MPMMixedEnvCfg`, `G1MPMMixedEnvCfg_PLAY` |
| `…/velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/streaming_mpm_mixed_env.py` (new, 246 lines) | `G1MPMMixedEnv` (per-environment foot margin, rigid foot force, sand reset for sand environments), `MixedMPMObject`, `randomize_mpm_material` for sand environments |
| `…/g1_29dof/mdp/terminations.py`, `mdp/__init__.pyi` | New term `root_out_of_patch` |
| `…/g1_29dof/__init__.py` | Registers the two task IDs |
| `…/g1_29dof/env_cfg/terrain_generator_cfg.py` | `ROUGH_TERRAINS_CFG`: `num_cols` 5 → 10; random rough `noise_range` (0.02, 0.10) → (0.02, 0.08). Both also apply to the sand-only task. |
| `…/g1_29dof/streaming_mpm_env.py`, `streaming_mpm_env_cfg.py` | Removed the unused sampled foot margin (`foot_contact_margin_range` and its code). No mixed-task code in the base task. |
| Newton, `~/isaac/worktrees/mpm-unification/newton`: `newton/_src/solvers/implicit_mpm/particle_cache.py`, `particle_streaming.py` (+16 −5 lines) | The tile cache accepts worlds that hold no particles; such a world gets no pool. Needed for the cache presets on the mixed task. |

`velocity_mpm/config/deprecated/` is untouched.

### Config values

| Value | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `scene.terrain.sand_env_fraction` | 0.5 | Share of environments on sand, in (0, 1]. A single environment is on sand when this is above 0.5. |
| `mpm_contact_margin` | 0.02 m | Foot margin in sand environments |
| `rigid_contact_margin` | 0.0 m | Foot margin in rigid environments |
| `terminations.out_of_patch.params.margin` | 0.5 m | Distance inside the patch edge at which the episode ends |
| Play reset range | ±3 m in x and y | Was ±5 m, which exceeds the 4 m half column |

### Commands

```
# Play: env 0 on sand, env 1 on rigid, both on the wave pair
uv run --with wandb isaaclab play --rl_library rsl_rl --task IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed-Play \
    --num_envs 2 --viz newton_rtx --checkpoint wandb:jnskkmr/g1_29dof_mpm_mixed_terrain/eayrj79r \
    physics=solver_cfg_cache "env.video_recorders=[]"

# One robot on sand
... --num_envs 1 env.scene.terrain.sand_env_fraction=1.0
```

A scene with no sand environment (for example `--num_envs 1` with the default fraction) is rejected with a
config error; use `--num_envs 2` to see a rigid robot.

### Results, measured in this session on OSMO (policy `eayrj79r`)

Streaming without tile cache (`t3-mixed-nosand-1`):

| Case | Environments holding sand | Sand force on rigid robots' feet | Sand robots on their sand: foot force, falls |
|---|---|---|---|
| Play, 10 robots, 60 s, `solver_cfg_no_cache` | 0–4 | 0.0 N | 335 N, 0 |
| Training config, 10 robots, 120 s, `solver_cfg_all_active_no_cache` | 0–4 | 0.0 N | 328 N, 0 |
| Play, 2 robots, 30 s | 0 | 0.0 N | 348 N, 0 |
| Play, 1 robot, `sand_env_fraction=1.0`, 30 s | 0 | n/a | 348 N, 0 |

Streaming with the tile cache, after the Newton change (`t3-mixed-cache-1`, `solver_cfg_cache`):

| Case | Environments holding sand | Sand force on rigid robots' feet | Sand robots: foot force, falls | Time on the other ground |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Play, 2 robots, 30 s (the viewer command, headless) | 0 | 0.0 N | 339 N, 0 | 0 % |
| Play, 10 robots, 60 s | 0–4 | 0.0 N | 305 N, 0 | 0 % |
| Training config, 10 robots, 120 s | 0–4 | 0.0 N | 341 N, 0 | 0 % |
| Play, 1 robot, `sand_env_fraction=1.0`, 30 s | 0 | n/a | 340 N, 0 | 0 % |
| Sand-only task, Play, 10 robots, 30 s (regression check) | all | n/a | 341 N, 0 falls, 2 out-of-bounds resets | n/a |

`root_out_of_patch` with the tile-grid ends ran in this test: no robot was on the other ground at any step.

Earlier checks on the same task:

- Sand robots that stay on their sand behave as in the sand-only task (0 resets in 10 robots × 15 s for both;
  sole 17–18 cm below the surface in Play for both, where the sand is 0.35 m of loose poppy seed).
- A 0 cm foot margin on sand is clearly worse (2 resets in 10, body tilt 6–12° against 3–5°), which is why a
  robot must not cross onto the other ground.
- Before the time-out, robots spent 35 % of Play time (23 % with the training reset range) on the wrong ground.
  With the side-of-column time-out, rigid robots spent 0 % on sand.
- Force blow-ups of 100–700 kN on rigid random rough terrain came from 8 cm vertical walls; with
  `noise_range=(0.02, 0.08)` the largest foot force was 4.4 kN with no resets.

### Not verified yet

- The viewer (`--viz newton_rtx`) with the tile cache on the mixed task; all tests ran headless.
- No training has been launched on this task.

### Open issues and decisions

1. **Episodes cut at the patch border.** In the 120 s training-config tests, 41 of 77 episodes (side borders
   only) and 54 of 82 episodes (sides and tile-grid ends) ended at the patch border instead of running the full
   20 s. With alternating 8 m columns the border is at most 3.5 m away on both
   sides. The legacy layout (rigid half and sand half, five types repeated in each half) would leave a single
   dividing line; it needs the five sub-terrains listed twice in `ROUGH_TERRAINS_CFG`. Not changed.
2. **Trainings to launch.** Planned: `physics=solver_cfg_all_active_no_cache` and `physics=solver_cfg_cache`, to
   compare against `eayrj79r` (no cache). Waiting for the go-ahead and the GPU count; the legacy run used
   40 processes × 64 environments.
3. **Robot link collision type.** `MPMSolver.prepare_builder` turns every convex robot link into a triangle mesh
   in all MPM-coupled tasks. Suspected to make rigid contact harsher; untested, and it is shared library code.
4. **Newton changes are committed but not pushed** on branch `jnskkmhr/mpm-unification` of the Newton worktree:
   `01d6e3d3` (leak fix) and `e681453d` (tile cache with sand-free worlds).
<!-- T3-mixed-terrain:end -->

<!-- T3-for-T1:start -->
## Notes for T1 from the mixed-terrain training (2026-11-21)

Found while launching `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed` trainings on OSMO (40 single-GPU L40 nodes x 64
environments, 32 of them on sand). The two Newton fixes are committed on branch `jnskkmhr/mpm-unification` of
`~/isaac/worktrees/mpm-unification/newton` (not pushed); the Isaac Lab changes are uncommitted.

### 1. Host-memory leak in the coupled solver (fixed in Newton commit `01d6e3d3`)

- **Symptom.** Training processes were killed with exit code 137 about 25 minutes after job start
  (`g1-mpm-mixed-cache-1` at iteration 12, `g1-mpm-mixed-no-cache-1` at iteration 36). Host memory of the training
  process grew about 4 GB per minute after the first iteration until it hit the 64 GB container limit. GPU memory
  was steady.
- **Cause.** `newton/_src/solvers/coupled/solver_coupled.py`. Every particle material change
  (`ModelFlags.MODEL_PROPERTIES`) makes the coupler project all particle attributes into each sub-solver view. For
  each attribute it builds a Python index list with one entry per particle (`_expanded_row_indices` copies the
  stored list) and `_indices_are_identity` keeps that list in `_identity_memo`, keyed by the list's `id`. A new
  list per call means a new entry per call, so the memo grew without bound. The memo was added in commit
  `fcd765f8` (2026-11-16).
- **Trigger.** Sand material randomization at reset (`mpm_material`, `mpm_material_log` events). It fires on
  every step in which an environment resets, which early in training is nearly every step.
- **Not affected.** Tasks without material randomization at reset: the speed-bench tasks and the tow tasks.
  Measured on one GPU with 64 environments: the sand-only walking task leaks at the same rate as the mixed task;
  with the two material events switched off it does not leak (host memory flat at 7-9 GB).
- **Not the cause.** The viewer and video recording, W&B, thread limits (`OMP_NUM_THREADS` and similar), the
  number of terrain columns, the mixed-task code.
- **Fix.** 7 lines in `solver_coupled.py`: `_expanded_row_indices` returns the stored list for row width 1
  instead of a copy, and `_identity_memo` is cleared when it reaches 256 entries. A CPU regression test
  (`test_model_properties_notifications_do_not_accumulate_index_lists` in `newton/tests/test_coupled_solver.py`)
  passes with the fix and fails without it (the memo grows from 50 to 175 entries over five notifications).
- **Result with the fix** (one GPU, 64 environments, material randomization on): host memory flat at 6-7.5 GB
  over 30 iterations without cache and at about 10 GB with cache.

### 2. Iteration time, unified against legacy

Earlier in this record the unified task was compared with the legacy run `eayrj79r` using its whole-run average of
10.6 s per iteration. That comparison was wrong: iteration time depends strongly on the training stage, because
resets are expensive and episodes are short at the start.

Legacy run `eayrj79r` (legacy moving patch without cache, 40 nodes x 64 environments, from W&B):

| Iterations | Mean episode length [steps] | Collection time [s] | Learning time [s] |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0-50 | 42 | 18.2 | 5.4 |
| 50-500 | 75 | 16.8 | 5.0 |
| 500-5000 | 509 | 8.5 | 3.3 |
| 5000-20000 | 902 | 6.6 | 2.6 |

Unified mixed task at the same stage (iterations 3-29, mean episode length about 44 steps, one GPU, 64 environments
of which 32 on sand, material randomization on, with the leak fix):

| Preset | Collection time [s] | Learning time [s] |
|---|---|---|
| `solver_cfg_no_cache` | 12.5 | 0.13 |
| `solver_cfg_cache` | 16.4 (40-43 while the first video is recorded) | 0.13 |
| `solver_cfg_no_cache`, before the leak fix | about 27 per iteration in total | |

- At the same training stage the unified streaming task without cache collects faster than the legacy run
  (12.5 s against 18.2 s). It is not slower.
- The learning time is not comparable: the unified numbers are from one node, the legacy numbers include the
  gradient synchronization across 40 nodes.
- **With the tile cache the mixed task is slower than without it** in this setting (16.4 s against 12.5 s
  collection). The cache preset holds 50,176 particle slots per sand environment against 20,000 without cache.
  This is early training with a reset about every 44 steps; it has not been measured for long episodes.
- The speed-bench configuration notes the same cost from the other side: with material randomization at reset an
  iteration took 35-45 s with 32 environments and 5 s without. That measurement predates the leak fix, so part of
  it was the leak.

### 3. Tile cache with worlds that hold no particles (Newton commit `e681453d`)

The mixed task gives sand only to the sand environments. `newton/_src/solvers/implicit_mpm/particle_cache.py`
required the same number of simulation slots in every world and raised otherwise. Changed (16 lines added, 5
removed, with `particle_streaming.py`): a world without particles gets no pool, and the equal-slots check applies
only to worlds that hold particles. Regression check on the sand-only task with `solver_cfg_cache`: 10 robots,
30 s, same sinkage and speed as before.

### 4. OSMO launcher for the unified stack

`osmo/run_multinode.sh` and `osmo/osmo_multi_gpu.yaml` were copied from `~/isaac/IsaacLab/osmo` into the unified
checkout and adapted (30 changed lines): the unified `uv.lock` installs Newton and Warp from the sibling folders
`../newton` and `../warp`, so the launcher uploads those folders and the workflow copies them next to the code.
It also copies the git-ignored robot `.usda` assets. Usage is unchanged.

### 5. Running trainings

`g1-mpm-mixed-cache-3` (`physics=solver_cfg_cache`, run name `train_scratch_cache`) and `g1-mpm-mixed-no-cache-3`
(`physics=solver_cfg_no_cache`, run name `train_scratch_no_cache`), W&B project `g1_29dof_mpm_mixed_terrain`,
submitted 2026-11-21 on `isaac-lab-l40s-03` with the fixes above. A four-node probe on that pool trained at
12.7-13.0 s per iteration without cache, the same as on L40.
<!-- T3-for-T1:end -->

## Requested changes to `tasks/T3.md`, for the lead session (2026-11-21)

`tasks/T3.md` is edited by the lead session only, so the changes Junnosuke decided in the T3 session on
2026-11-21 are recorded here. Items marked "decided" are his; items marked "suggested" are from the T3 session and
need his or the lead's confirmation.

### 1. Fold P4 into P1 and P2 (decided)

P4 ("Where do particles not help?", the P1 metrics on rigid ground, step 5) stops being a row of its own. The
question is answered inside P1 and P2, as an analysis along two directions:

- **Terrain geometry (in P1).** On flat and gently sloped ground every policy succeeds, so training on particles
  does not matter much there. It starts to matter on steeper slopes and for walking that is not straight.
- **Terrain material (in P2).** On hard ground training on particles does not matter much. It starts to matter as
  the ground gets softer: the baselines' tracking error rises and their step time shortens, while the MPM policy
  stays steady (the density-friction grid figure, Fig. 4 of the T3 slides).

Requested edits:

| Where in `tasks/T3.md` | Change |
|---|---|
| Acceptance table, row P1 | Add: "Report where the policies do not differ (flat and gentle slopes) as well as where they do." Add the walking directions of item 2 below. |
| Acceptance table, row P2 | Add: "Report along the material axis from hard to soft, including hard ground where the policies do not differ." Add step time to the metrics. |
| Acceptance table, row P4 | Remove. |
| Steps, step 5 ("Rigid ground") | Remove as a step of its own; rigid ground becomes the hard end of the material axis in step 3. |
| Slides list, "P4: the same policies on rigid ground" | Remove. |
| Row P5 | Renumber if the lead wants consecutive numbers. |

### 2. Walking directions on slopes (decided)

P1 so far evaluates straight walking only. Add sideways, diagonal and circular walking on slopes on top of straight
walking. Not implemented yet. Before fixing the commands, the T3 session will check each policy's training command
ranges so that every evaluated command is inside all three.

### 3. Axes and what each depends on (decided)

| Axis | Content | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | Generalization to rougher terrain: slopes, and walking directions on slopes | The MPM policy retrained on every terrain type (item 5) |
| P2 | Performance under different terrain material, from hard to soft | The same policies |
| P3 | Soil interaction with an object | T2's object towing. `tasks/T3.md` still says "object pushing"; T2's task is now towing. |

### 4. Hardware (decided, with one suggestion)

- Hardware remains the deciding comparison and applies to P1 to P3.
- For the initial submission what hardware can show is limited: on flat ground and on a 10 degree slope every
  policy succeeded. Step times Junnosuke measured on hardware (flat / 10 degrees): MPM 0.33 / 0.34 s,
  RFT 0.42 / 0.39 s, MJWarp 0.39 / 0.36 s.
- Decided: try a steeper slope, about 20 degrees, to see whether a gap appears.
- Suggested, from the simulated sweep below: 20 degrees should separate MPM from MJWarp on success and from RFT on
  tracking error; separating RFT on success needs about 30 degrees, so 25 degrees is probably the practical upper
  end on dry sand. A downhill test with the current MPM policy would look bad (see item 5).

Simulated uphill sweep on MPM sand at 1.0 m/s, 10 trials per cell, measured in this session (MPM `eayrj79r`,
RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MJWarp `3qymp37c`):

| +15 deg | 10 | 10 | 10 | 0.08 / 0.11 / 0.06 |
| +20 deg | 7 | 10 | 10 | 0.13 / 0.13 / 0.06 |
| +25 deg | 6 | 10 | 10 | 0.21 / 0.24 / 0.08 |

### 5. MPM-Rough is being retrained (decided)

- Reason: the current MPM policy `eayrj79r` came from the legacy mixed training, where the sand robots only saw
  wave and random rough terrain; both pyramid slopes and flat were rigid only. Downhill on sand it tracks worst
  (velocity error 0.52 m/s at -20 degrees against 0.10 m/s for both baselines) and reaches 7 of 10 goals at
  -30 degrees.
- New training: `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed` on the unified code, every terrain type once as rigid
  ground and once as sand. Rewards unchanged from `eayrj79r`, so that a difference can be attributed to terrain
  coverage. Details in "Mixed MJWarp/MPM terrain task on the unified code" above.
- Jobs, submitted 2026-11-21 on `isaac-lab-l40s-03`, 40 nodes x 64 environments, 20,000 iterations:
  `g1-mpm-mixed-cache-3` (`physics=solver_cfg_cache`, run name `train_scratch_cache`) and
  `g1-mpm-mixed-no-cache-3` (`physics=solver_cfg_no_cache`, run name `train_scratch_no_cache`), W&B project
  `g1_29dof_mpm_mixed_terrain`. Result pending; `eayrj79r` took 59 hours.
- `tasks/T3.md` says "MPM-Rough is the policy retrained in T2 step 1". The lead should say which run is MPM-Rough
  once these finish.

### 6. Points the lead should know when rewriting the task (from the T3 session)

- **RFT baseline.** RFT-Rough is `6t0h2ud8` (rigid support 0 to 0.40 m below the sand). Junnosuke decided on
  2026-11-20 not to add a traction heuristic to RFT.
- **P2 does not separate the policies on success.** All three reached the goal on 50 of 50 random sands and 210
  of 210 grid sands. The separation is in tracking error (MPM 0.08 m/s, RFT 0.12 m/s) and in step time. P2's
  metrics in the task file (success rate, pelvis jerk, joint smoothness) should include these two.
- **Baselines are not from one commit.** `3qymp37c` and `6t0h2ud8` were trained in the old worktree, not from the
  unified commit the task file requires. Not decided whether to retrain them.
- **Terrain shapes outside the training set** (P1) have not been evaluated; only slopes.
- **Rigid-ground results are incomplete.** Step time on rigid ground was measured with the old RFT policy, not
  `6t0h2ud8`, and the rigid downhill evaluation is broken (every policy 0 of 10 at -10 degrees). Both need
  redoing before rigid ground is used as the hard end of the material axis.
- **Pelvis-height reward on sand.** The target is 0.50 m above the sand surface against 0.75 m on rigid ground,
  in `eayrj79r` and in the new trainings. Junnosuke decided on 2026-11-21 to leave it unchanged for this
  comparison.

### Goal reaching

Goal: the robot's path (its horizontal speed summed over time) reaches 10 m before the time limit (40 s at 0.5 m/s, 20 s at 1 m/s); it is a path length, not a distance from the start. Fall: any termination, which is a pelvis tilt above 45° or the pelvis-height term. Time limit: trials that neither fell nor reached the goal. Time to goal: mean ± std over the trials that reached it. The remaining columns are mean ± std over the trials without a fall, each averaged from 5 s to its end (after the start-up of the tow), and empty when fewer than three trials stayed up. Forward speed error: mean absolute difference between the base-frame forward velocity and the command; the base frame leans with the pelvis, so the robot speed (horizontal, world frame) is given beside it. Yaw-rate error: mean absolute difference between the instantaneous base yaw rate and the command; it includes the yaw oscillation of each step. Tether tension and horizontal tether pull are the magnitude and the horizontal part of the same logged tether force; averaged over a trial the horizontal force of the ground on the cube equals the pull, so these are one measurement, not two. The cube speed is given because the cube does not move at the commanded speed on every ground. Cube lower than at rest: height of the cube while it rests before the tether goes taut (0.2 to 2 s, tension below 5 N, speed below 0.02 m/s) minus its height while towed, per trial; it needs no surface level. With the turning command the robot walks a circle of 1 m radius, shorter than the 1.2 m tether, so the cube stays near the centre and pivots: those rows test turning while tied to the cube, not dragging it along a curve.

### Bed depth, MPM-trained policy on MPM sand

### What the numbers show

- trained on rigid reaches the goal in 48 of 48 on rigid ground, 4 of 48 on RFT sand, 0 of 48 on MPM sand.
- trained on rigid + RFT reaches the goal in 48 of 48 on rigid ground, 48 of 48 on RFT sand, 0 of 48 on MPM sand.
- trained on rigid + MPM reaches the goal in 48 of 48 on rigid ground, 43 of 48 on RFT sand, 48 of 48 on MPM sand.
- Trials without a fall with the tether cut, 0.5 m/s command, on rigid ground: trained on rigid 16 of 16, trained on rigid + RFT 16 of 16, trained on rigid + MPM 16 of 16; on RFT sand: trained on rigid 0 of 16, trained on rigid + RFT 16 of 16, trained on rigid + MPM 16 of 16; on MPM sand: trained on rigid 0 of 16, trained on rigid + RFT 0 of 16, trained on rigid + MPM 16 of 16.
- Trials without a fall with the tether cut, 1 m/s command, on rigid ground: trained on rigid 16 of 16, trained on rigid + RFT 16 of 16, trained on rigid + MPM 16 of 16; on RFT sand: trained on rigid 0 of 16, trained on rigid + RFT 16 of 16, trained on rigid + MPM 9 of 16; on MPM sand: trained on rigid 0 of 16, trained on rigid + RFT 0 of 16, trained on rigid + MPM 2 of 16.
- Force on the cube at the same cube speed (the 0.5 m/s command, cube at 0.45 to 0.49 m/s): 50 N ± 5 N on rigid ground, 62 N on matched RFT sand (RFT-trained policy; the MPM-trained policy drags the cube at only 0.18 m/s there) and 103 N on MPM sand. This is the like-for-like comparison. At the 1.0 m/s command the cube moves at 0.99 m/s on rigid ground, 0.97 m/s on RFT sand and 0.67 m/s on MPM sand, so the 124 N on MPM sand is not at the speed of the other two and is not compared with them.
- The policies trained on rigid ground and on rigid + RFT do not get as far as steady walking on this MPM sand: 0 of 48 goals, and every trial ends within 1.5 s of the start, with the tether and with it cut. This is not the randomized start. From a start at rest (which still drops the robot about 9.4 cm onto the sand top, as every start on this bed does) the rigid-trained policy falls after 0.84 to 1.42 s and the RFT-trained one after 0.88 to 1.72 s (64 trials each). After standing for 2 s with a zero command, the RFT-trained policy is still up in all 64 trials (tilt at 1.9 s: mean 7°, up to 21°) and falls 0.80 to 1.98 s after the command starts; the rigid-trained policy is already tilting during the stand (mean 13°, up to 22°) and falls 0.00 to 1.28 s after the command (12 of 64 trials in under 0.8 s). So their failure is neither a towing-force result nor a reset result.
- The tether-cut contrast between the three policies is the 0.5 m/s row: on MPM sand the MPM-trained policy stays up in 16 of 16 trials and the other two in 0 of 16. The 1.0 m/s cut row is not a clean walking test for the MPM-trained policy: without the cube it runs at 1.3 m/s with a forward lean of 37 to 38° (with the cube: 0.67 m/s and 20°), close to the 45° tilt limit, and stays up in 15, 9 and 2 of 16 trials on the 0.25, 0.35 and 0.45 m beds (0 of 16 from a start at rest, 3 of 16 after standing 2 s). On sand it was trained only with a cube of 5 to 15 kg.
- Is tow training what removed walking on sand? The walking policies the tow policies were started from, run in this tow evaluation with the tether cut on the 0.45 m bed (16 trials per cell, start as in training; logs `logs/t2/tow_walk_d45`): the RFT walking policy `6t0h2ud8` stays up in 16 of 16 at 0.5 and at 1.0 m/s and walks at 0.46 and 1.01 m/s, feet 12 cm or more above the base, while the tow policy trained from it stays up in 0 of 16. So for the RFT-trained policy, tow training on flat ground did remove walking on this sand. The rigid walking policy `3qymp37c` stays up in only 6 of 16 at 0.5 m/s and 0 of 16 at 1.0 m/s on this bed (falls after 4.2 to 18.9 s and 1.6 to 14.9 s; its feet sink to within 2 cm of the base in 6 and 9 of 16 trials), so for the rigid-trained policy most of the failure is already in the walking policy on deep loose sand and cannot be put on tow training; its earlier 16 of 16 was on the 0.25 m bed. The MPM walking policy `eayrj79r` stays up in 16 of 16 at both speeds.
- Bed depth changes the towing numbers of the MPM-trained policy, which was trained on the 0.25 m bed where its feet reach the base: horizontal pull at the 1.0 m/s command 105 N, 120 N and 124 N on the 0.25, 0.35 and 0.45 m beds, with the cube moving at 0.92, 0.71 and 0.67 m/s. Goal reaching is 16 of 16 at every depth.
- On rigid ground the pull depends on the direction of travel (42 to 56 N over the random headings, cube and ground friction 0.5), which is what a pyramidal friction cone gives; the ± 5 N of the rigid rows is this heading dependence, not trial noise.
- Gait of the MPM-trained policy against the ground (MPM sands on the 0.45 m bed; lowest sole corner 7.4 cm or more above the base in every sand cell). From rigid ground to the three MPM sands the tether tension rises from 60 N to 93 to 136 N and the torso lean from 7 to 9° to 17 to 23°, and the pelvis moves from 14 to 15 cm to 22 to 32 cm ahead of the stance foot. Within the three MPM sands the lean does not follow the tension: it stays between 17 and 23° (the lean penalty starts at 20°) while the tension goes from 93 to 136 N; the pelvis lead does keep growing with the tension (22, 22, 28 cm at 0.5 m/s; 27, 28, 32 cm at 1.0 m/s). On matched RFT sand, outside its training, the policy does not fit the trend: at the 0.5 m/s command it leans 3° and drags the cube at 0.17 m/s. So the lean grows with the required pull between rigid ground and sand, but it saturates near the allowed lean and is not a smooth function of the pull.
- Baselines, on the grounds where they stay up: the RFT-trained policy leans more on RFT sand than on rigid ground (5.8° to 9.6° at 0.5 m/s, 6.2° to 13.8° at 1.0 m/s, tension 59 to 60 N against 73 N), the same direction as the MPM-trained policy. The rigid-trained policy stays up only on rigid ground, so it gives one point per speed.
- Earlier deformation: around the circle the pull falls from 96 N in lap 1 to 88 N in lap 3 (8% lower) while the cube runs 2 cm deeper in its own trench, at a cube speed of 0.40 m/s; a straight tow over fresh sand needs 104 N at 0.47 m/s.

### Not done and remaining differences

- The MPM-trained tow policy was trained on a 0.25 m bed, where its feet come within 2 cm of the rigid base in 16 of 16 towing trials; it is evaluated here on a 0.45 m bed, which it has not seen. It was not retrained on the deeper bed.
- Shared recipe: 1,280 environments, 20,000 tow iterations, 15 kg cube with a mass curriculum from 5 kg on half of the environments (the soft-ground half where there is one), forward lean up to 20° unpenalized, seed 42.
- Differences between the three trainings that remain: (1) mirror loss on with coefficient 0.1 for the rigid and RFT policies, off for the MPM policy (mirror data augmentation on in all three); (2) the PPO runner: compositional for MPM, standard RSL-RL for the others; (3) Newton commit `7adb5be70` with 2 uncommitted entries for the MPM training, `e681453df` clean for the other two; (4) reset position range ±5 m in the RFT task, ±0.5 m in the other two; (5) observation and reward functions follow each task's walking task (base height and foot clearance against the height scanner on MPM, against world height on rigid and RFT); (6) the rigid task's config still holds its walking task's RFT randomization events, which point at a disabled term and do nothing; (7) ground material: RFT default (3,000 kg/m³, 0.3, 0.3) against loose poppy seed on a 0.25 m bed. Sources: the task packages and the bundle logs `logs/t2/osmo/bundle10.log` (MPM) and `bundle11.log` (rigid, RFT).
- All three tow tasks are flat, while the walking policies they start from were trained on rough terrain.
- The RFT-trained tow policy was trained on the RFT task's default material and is tested on RFT sand with the MPM sand's density, internal friction and surface friction; it was not retrained on that material. RFT's generic coefficient table, its contact threshold and its flat, non-deforming surface have no MPM counterpart and are unchanged.
- The cube's rest level is read in the 0.2 to 2 s after it is dropped 2 cm onto the ground, while the tether is still slack; that is 2 to 38 samples per trial. With the tether cut the 0.1 kg cube keeps sinking slowly in the MPM sand, so no static depth of the cube is quoted.
- One training seed per policy. Two evaluation seeds (42, 43) of 8 trials each. One sand material for the comparison, not randomized. One cube. No hardware.
- Provenance: the tow tasks were committed during these runs (IsaacLab `0007b2e67`); before that they were untracked files. The evaluation scripts (`scripts/benchmarks/t2/tow_check.py`, `tow_merge.py`, `tow_report.py`, `tow_gait.py`, `tow_base_clearance.py`) are uncommitted and `tow_check.py` was edited between batches (start options and an early stop were added; the trial loop is otherwise unchanged). `tow_d25`, `tow_d35` and `tow_rft_matched` ran before that edit, the other folders after it. Two code states appear inside single tables: runs before the commit of the tow tasks are on IsaacLab `a60612c79` and Newton `e681453df`, later ones on `0007b2e67` and `48231a989`; the per-folder list below names which. A copy of the scripts and tasks as they stand after the last run, with the diff and status of both repositories, is in `logs/t2/code_snapshot_20261124/`. Each run's JSON holds the commit and tree state of IsaacLab and Newton at the time it ran.
- Videos of the three final checkpoints are not recorded.

## P2 object towing: why the rigid and RFT tow policies fall on sand (T2 session, 2026-11-21)

**Partly superseded, 2026-11-24** (after the independent check, `checks/T2.md`). Step 2 below used earlier tow runs (`g1-tow-rigid-2`, `g1-tow-soft-contact-1`, MPM checkpoint 21,500) on the 0.25 m bed, without foot positions. The tether-cut control and a start-condition control on the three evaluated final checkpoints, on a 0.45 m bed, are in the block "P2 object towing (from the T2 tow policies)" above; use those. Step 1 (the walking policies walk on this sand) was measured on the 0.25 m bed, where T3 later found the walking feet close to the rigid base. Repeated on the 0.45 m bed in the tow evaluation with the tether cut (`logs/t2/tow_walk_d45`, findings of the block above): the RFT walking policy `6t0h2ud8` and the MPM walking policy `eayrj79r` stay up in 16 of 16 at 0.5 and 1.0 m/s; the rigid walking policy `3qymp37c` in 6 of 16 and 0 of 16. So "the walking policy it was started from can walk on the sand" holds for the RFT policy and not for the rigid one on a deep bed. The sentence "cannot walk on the sand at all" below is too strong for what was measured: the tow policies trained on rigid ground and on rigid + RFT fall within 1.5 s of starting to walk, from every start that was tried.

Status: measured in a T2 session, unchecked. Asked for by the lead (steps 1 and 2 of its reply to the towing handoff). Code: IsaacLab `mpm-unification` worktree at `a60612c79`, dirty tree; Newton `e681453df`, clean. GPU: one RTX 5090. Sand depth 0.25 m, voxel 0.04 m (the T3 walking evaluation uses 0.02 m), streaming MPM with the tile cache.

Step 1. Walking policies on flat MPM sand, plain walking task, no object: `scripts/benchmarks/t3/eval_walk.py --angle 0 --terrain ramp --voxel 0.04 --depth 0.25 --num_trials 10 --seconds 13 --travel 5 --seed 41` (`IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-Play`, `physics=solver_cfg_cache`). Start state: position within 0.1 m, heading within 0.1 rad, zero velocity, joint angles scaled 0.9 to 1.1. Raw data `logs/t2/walk_check/`. Velocity error: planar world-frame error after the first second, mean ± std over trials.

| Walking policy | Sand | Command | Goal reached / trials | Falls | Velocity error [m/s] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MJWarp-Rough `3qymp37c` | poppy_seed_loose (640 kg/m3, friction 0.73) | 0.5 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.161 ± 0.018 |
| MJWarp-Rough `3qymp37c` | poppy_seed_loose | 1.0 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.185 ± 0.019 |
| MJWarp-Rough `3qymp37c` | sand (1800 kg/m3, friction 0.80, the T3 evaluation sand) | 0.5 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.064 ± 0.008 |
| MJWarp-Rough `3qymp37c` | sand | 1.0 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.098 ± 0.023 |
| RFT-Rough `6t0h2ud8` | poppy_seed_loose | 0.5 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.087 ± 0.008 |
| RFT-Rough `6t0h2ud8` | poppy_seed_loose | 1.0 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.125 ± 0.016 |

| RFT-Rough `6t0h2ud8` | sand | 1.0 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.127 ± 0.018 |
| MPM-Rough `eayrj79r` | poppy_seed_loose | 0.5 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.053 ± 0.011 |
| MPM-Rough `eayrj79r` | poppy_seed_loose | 1.0 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.079 ± 0.018 |
| MPM-Rough `eayrj79r` | sand | 0.5 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.055 ± 0.007 |
| MPM-Rough `eayrj79r` | sand | 1.0 m/s | 10 / 10 | 0 | 0.085 ± 0.021 |

Step 2. The tow evaluation setup with the rope cut: `scripts/benchmarks/t2/tow_check.py --masses 0 (x16) --seconds 20 --ground sand` (`IsaacContrib-Tow-G1-29dof-MPM-Play`, poppy_seed_loose, the cube lies on the sand untied). "Random start" adds `--randomize`: position within 0.5 m, any heading, linear and angular velocity within 0.5, joint angles scaled 0.5 to 1.5. "Fixed start" is the play configuration's fixed pose. No fall / 16 trials, at 0.5 and 1.0 m/s. Raw data `logs/t2/tow_eval/*-norope*`.

| Policy | Start | No fall at 0.5 m/s | No fall at 1.0 m/s |
|---|---|---|---|
| Walking, MJWarp-Rough `3qymp37c` | random | 16 / 16 | 16 / 16 |
| Walking, MJWarp-Rough `3qymp37c` | fixed | 16 / 16 | 16 / 16 |
| Walking, RFT-Rough `6t0h2ud8` | random | 16 / 16 | 16 / 16 |
| Walking, RFT-Rough `6t0h2ud8` | fixed | 16 / 16 | 16 / 16 |
| Walking, MPM-Rough `eayrj79r` | random | 16 / 16 | 16 / 16 |
| Tow, trained on rigid (`g1-tow-rigid-2`, 39,998) | random | 0 / 16 | 0 / 16 |
| Tow, trained on rigid | fixed | 0 / 16 | 0 / 16 |
| Tow, trained on rigid + RFT (`g1-tow-soft-contact-1`, 39,998) | random | 12 / 16 | 0 / 16 |
| Tow, trained on rigid + RFT | fixed | 16 / 16 | 0 / 16 |
| Tow, trained on rigid + MPM (`g1-tow-mpm-mixed-6`, 21,500) | random | 16 / 16 | 16 / 16 |

What this shows:
- The walking policies walk on this sand in both setups, with either start. The start randomization, the material and the voxel size are not the cause of the falls.
- The rigid tow policy cannot walk on the sand at all, although the walking policy it was started from can. Its 20,000 iterations of tow training on flat rigid ground removed that ability. The RFT tow policy keeps it at 0.5 m/s and loses it at 1.0 m/s.
- The only cell where a baseline walks without the cube and fails with it is the RFT tow policy at 0.5 m/s (16 / 16 without the cube, fixed start; 0 / 16 with the 15 kg cube). That is the one towing failure in this comparison that can be attributed to the cube.
- The walking policies were trained on rough terrain; the rigid and RFT tow tasks are flat only. This difference is not removed by giving all three the same cube-mass schedule and lean penalty.

## P2 object towing: rigid and RFT tow policies retrained with the MPM recipe (T2 session, 2026-11-22)

Owner request: retrain the rigid and RFT tow policies with the same cube-mass curriculum and forward-lean allowance as the MPM tow policy. Submitted 2026-11-22 02:21 PST. All three runs finished at iteration 39,998; W&B `jnskkmr/object_tow`: rigid `aqiwzrv0`, rigid + RFT `9733ux8d`, rigid + MPM `her588wk`. Their evaluation at the final checkpoint is in the block "P2 object towing (from the T2 tow policies)" above. Corrected 2026-11-24 after the independent check (`checks/T2.md`): the code line and the list of differences below.

| | Rigid | Rigid + RFT | Rigid + MPM (reference, already running) |
|---|---|---|---|
| OSMO job | `g1-tow-rigid-3` | `g1-tow-soft-contact-2` | `g1-tow-mpm-mixed-6` (W&B `her588wk`) |
| Task | `IsaacContrib-Tow-G1-29dof-Rigid` | `IsaacContrib-Tow-G1-29dof-SoftContact` | `IsaacContrib-Tow-G1-29dof-MPM-Mixed` |
| Warm start | `3qymp37c` | `6t0h2ud8` | `eayrj79r` |
| Environments | 4 GPUs x 320 = 1,280 | 4 GPUs x 320 = 1,280 | 40 GPUs x 32 = 1,280 |
| Iterations | 20,000 | 20,000 | 20,000 |
| Ground | flat rigid | flat, half rigid, half RFT | flat, half rigid, half MPM sand |
| Cube mass | half of the environments 5 to 15 kg by curriculum, half fixed 15 kg | RFT half 5 to 15 kg by curriculum, rigid half fixed 15 kg | sand half 5 to 15 kg by curriculum, rigid half fixed 15 kg |
| Orientation penalty | forward lean up to 20 degrees free | same | same |

Code, from the bundle logs `logs/t2/osmo/bundle11.log` (rigid, RFT) and `bundle10.log` (MPM): rigid and RFT runs IsaacLab `a60612c79` with 67 uncommitted entries, Newton `e681453df`, clean; MPM run IsaacLab `a60612c79` with 66 uncommitted entries, Newton `7adb5be70` with 2 uncommitted entries (two commits before `e681453df`: the memo fix in the coupled attribute projection and the tile cache skipping worlds without particles came after). Warp `3d0f4e5b7`, clean, for all. The curriculum term and the lean reward are the MPM tow task's functions (`g1_29dof_mpm_tow/mdp`), used by all three tasks. Seed 42 for all three (the config default; no `--seed` was passed).

Differences that remain:
- Terrain: all three tow tasks are flat. The walking policies they start from were trained on rough terrain. The earlier flat rigid tow policy lost its ability to walk on MPM sand (section above); this retrain does not address that.
- PPO runner: the MPM task uses the compositional runner; the rigid and RFT tasks the standard RSL-RL runner with their walking tasks' settings.
- Observations and rewards follow each task's walking task (for example the base height is measured against the height scanner on MPM and against world height on rigid and RFT); only the orientation penalty and the cube-mass schedule were made the same.
- RFT sand is one fixed default material (critical density 3,000 kg/m3, internal friction 0.3, surface friction 0.3); MPM sand is loose poppy seed (640 kg/m3, 0.73) on a 0.25 m bed. Neither is randomized. The rigid task's config still holds the RFT randomization events of its walking task, but they point at a disabled RFT term and do nothing.
- Mirror loss: on with coefficient 0.1 for the rigid and RFT runs, off for the MPM run. Mirror data augmentation is on in all three. Read from `g1_29dof_{rigid,soft_contact}_tow/agents/rsl_rl_ppo_cfg.py:51-53` and `g1_29dof_mpm_tow/agents/compositional_ppo_cfg.py:59`.
- Reset position range: within 5 m for the RFT task, within 0.5 m for the rigid and MPM tasks (`env_cfg/event_cfg.py` of each package).
- Newton commit: `7adb5be70` with uncommitted changes for the MPM training, `e681453df` for the other two and for every evaluation.
- One seed each (42).
<!-- T2-TOW-RETRAIN:end -->
